{'role': 'user', 'content': [{'type': 'text', 'text': '<file name="D:\\dev\\ab-tasks\\_briefs\\anyio\\brief.md">\n# Task: fix a reported bug in `anyio`\r\n\r\n## Bug report (verbatim from the project\'s issue tracker)\r\n**CapacityLimiter can over-grant tokens (`borrowed_tokens > total_tokens`, `available_tokens` goes negative)**\r\n\r\n### Things to check first\r\n\r\n- [x] I have searched the existing issues and didn\'t find my bug already reported there\r\n\r\n- [x] I have checked that my bug is still present in the latest release\r\n\r\n\r\n### AnyIO version\r\n\r\n4.13.0\r\n\r\n### Python version\r\n\r\n3.12\r\n\r\n### What happened?\r\n\r\nOn the asyncio backend, a non-blocking acquire can succeed *while the just-freed token is already reserved for a woken waiter*, leaving the limiter with more borrowers than it has tokens. The state becomes:\r\n\r\n```\r\nborrowed_tokens  = 2\r\ntotal_tokens     = 1\r\navailable_tokens = -1      # negative\r\nborrowers        = (\'B\', \'X\')\r\n```\r\n\r\nTwo tasks then hold a single-token limiter concurrently — the exact condition the limiter exists to prevent — and `available_tokens` is negative.\r\n\r\nThis violates the documented contract of `acquire_nowait` / `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait`:\r\n\r\n> :raises ~anyio.WouldBlock: if there are no tokens available for borrowing\r\n\r\nIn the window below there is no token available (the freed one is earmarked for the woken waiter), yet the nowait acquire succeeds instead of raising `WouldBlock`.\r\n\r\n### How can we reproduce the bug?\r\n\r\nJust anyio, no test framework: `python repro.py` prints the corrupt state and raises `AssertionError`:\r\n\r\n```python\r\nimport anyio\r\nfrom anyio import CapacityLimiter, WouldBlock\r\n\r\nasync def main():\r\n    lim = CapacityLimiter(1)                       # capacity: exactly ONE token\r\n    lim.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("A")           # A borrows the only token\r\n\r\n    async with anyio.create_task_group() as tg:\r\n        async def b():\r\n            await lim.acquire_on_behalf_of("B")    # B waits for the token\r\n        tg.start_soon(b)\r\n\r\n        while lim.statistics().tasks_waiting != 1:  # wait until B is parked\r\n            await anyio.sleep(0)\r\n\r\n        lim.release_on_behalf_of("A")              # frees the token, notifies B\r\n        # B has been notified but has NOT resumed yet, so it is not in\r\n        # _borrowers and the wait queue is now empty. A non-blocking acquire\r\n        # in this window slips through:\r\n        try:\r\n            lim.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("X")  # expected: WouldBlock\r\n            print("X acquired without waiting (should have raised WouldBlock)")\r\n        except WouldBlock:\r\n            print("X correctly blocked")\r\n        # leaving the task group lets B resume and add itself to _borrowers\r\n\r\n    print(f"borrowed_tokens  = {lim.borrowed_tokens}  (total_tokens = {lim.total_tokens})")\r\n    print(f"available_tokens = {lim.available_tokens}")\r\n    print(f"borrowers        = {lim.statistics().borrowers}")\r\n    assert lim.borrowed_tokens <= lim.total_tokens, "borrowed_tokens > total_tokens"\r\n\r\nanyio.run(main)\r\n```\r\n\r\nOutput:\r\n\r\n```\r\nX acquired without waiting (should have raised WouldBlock)\r\nborrowed_tokens  = 2  (total_tokens = 1)\r\navailable_tokens = -1\r\nborrowers        = (\'B\', \'X\')\r\nAssertionError: borrowed_tokens > total_tokens\r\n```\r\n### Cause\r\n\r\nIn `anyio/_backends/_asyncio.py`:\r\n\r\n- `release_on_behalf_of` removes the borrower then calls `_notify_next_waiter()`, which **pops** the next waiter out of `_wait_queue` and does `event.set()`.\r\n- The woken waiter, in `acquire_on_behalf_of`, only adds itself to `_borrowers` **after** `await event.wait()` returns:\r\n\r\n  ```python\r\n  await event.wait()\r\n  ...\r\n  self._borrowers.add(borrower)   # runs only once the waiter resumes\r\n  ```\r\n\r\n- Between the `event.set()` and the waiter resuming, `_wait_queue` is empty and the waiter is not yet in `_borrowers`. So the nowait guard\r\n\r\n  ```python\r\n  if self._wait_queue or len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens:\r\n      raise WouldBlock\r\n  ```\r\n\r\n  evaluates to `False` (empty queue, `0 < 1`) and admits a second borrower for the one token.\r\n\r\n### Why `Semaphore` is not affected\r\n\r\nThe sibling `Semaphore` is immune under the identical scenario, a nowait acquire issued in the same window correctly raises `WouldBlock`, because its `release()` hands the freed slot to the woken waiter rather than leaving it generally available. `CapacityLimiter` is the one primitive whose freed-but-reserved token is briefly visible as "available" to a synchronous acquirer.\r\n\r\nA fix would close that window, e.g. by reserving the token for the woken waiter at notify time (add the borrower to `_borrowers` when `_notify_next_waiter` sets its event, rather than after it resumes), mirroring the Semaphore/Lock hand-off. The nowait guard would then correctly see no free token.\r\n\r\n## Standing instructions (identical for every run)\r\nYou are working in the repository at the current working directory. Work like a careful open-source contributor:\r\n1. Reproduce the bug first (write a minimal failing test or script) before changing code.\r\n2. Find the root cause; fix the cause, not the symptom. Keep the change minimal and in the project\'s style.\r\n3. Add or update a regression test that fails before your fix and passes after.\r\n4. Run the project\'s test suite and make it green. Do not modify or delete unrelated tests.\r\n5. When done, summarize: root cause, the files you changed, how you verified it.\r\nDo not ask me for permission between steps; proceed until the task is complete, then report.\r\n\r\nTest command for this repo: `.venv/Scripts/python -m pytest tests/test_synchronization.py -q`\r\n\n</file>\n'}], 'attribution': 'user', 'timestamp': 1787265642786}

{'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Okay, now I understand the structure. Trio\'s backend wraps trio\'s CapacityLimiter, which correctly handles this (trio adds the borrower to `borrowed_tokens` at the point of notification).\n\nThe asyncio backend has this bug. The fix proposed in the bug report: reserve the token at notification time — that is, when `_notify_next_waiter` pops a waiter and sets its event, it should also add that borrower to `_borrowers` (and remove them from the wait queue). Then in `acquire_on_behalf_of`, after `event.wait()` returns, the borrower is already in `_borrowers`, so we shouldn\'t add again (or add is idempotent since it\'s a set, but we need to be careful about the exception path).\n\nLet me think carefully about the exception path:\n\nCurrent code:\n```python\nasync def acquire_on_behalf_of(self, borrower: object) -> None:\n    await AsyncIOBackend.checkpoint_if_cancelled()\n    try:\n        self.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait(borrower)\n    except WouldBlock:\n        event = asyncio.Event()\n        self._wait_queue[borrower] = event\n        try:\n            await event.wait()\n        except BaseException:\n            self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)\n            if event.is_set():\n                self._notify_next_waiter()\n\n            raise\n\n        self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n    else:\n        try:\n            await AsyncIOBackend.cancel_shielded_checkpoint()\n        except BaseException:\n            self.release()\n            raise\n```\n\nKey considerations for the fix:\n\n1. When `_notify_next_waiter` pops the waiter and sets the event, we should also `self._borrowers.add(borrower)`. That way `borrowed_tokens` reflects the reservation, `available_tokens` decreases, and the nowait guard `len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens` correctly raises WouldBlock.\n\n2. After `event.wait()` returns normally, the borrower is already in `_borrowers` (since we reserved at notification time). So we should NOT `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` again... actually, adding to a set is idempotent, so `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` after wait is harmless. But wait — is there a case where the event is set but the borrower was NOT reserved? The event can only be set via `_notify_next_waiter` (which, under the new scheme, reserves). So after a successful `event.wait()`, the borrower is always in `_borrowers`. Leaving the `.add()` is a harmless no-op. Hmm, but is it really? {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'now I\'ve got an anchored snapshot. Applying the fix:\n\n**Edit 1 — `total_tokens` setter (lines 2078–2082):** when granting a token to a waiter, reserve it:\n\n```python\n        # Notify waiting tasks that they have acquired the limiter\n        while self._wait_queue and waiters_to_notify:\n            borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)\n            self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n            event.set()\n            waiters_to_notify -= 1\n```\n\n**Edit 2 — `_notify_next_waiter` (lines 2092–2096):**\n\n```python\n    def _notify_next_waiter(self) -> None:\n        """Notify the next task in line if this limiter has free capacity now."""\n        if self._wait_queue and len(self._borrowers) < self._total_tokens:\n            borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)\n            # Reserve the token for the woken task immediately so that\n            # non-blocking acquires cannot steal it before it resumes\n            self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n            event.set()\n```\n\n**Edit 3 — `acquire_on_behalf_of` (lines 2122–2131):** exception path: if the event was set, we hold a reserved token, so release it before re-raising so it can be handed to the next waiter:\n\n```python\n            try:\n                await event.wait()\n            except BaseException:\n                self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)\n                if event.is_set():\n                    # We were granted a token but cancelled before returning\n                    # to the caller. Release the token and hand it to the\n                    # next waiter\n                    self._borrowers.remove(borrower)\n                    self._notify_next_waiter()\n\n                raise\n\n            self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n```\n\nWait — I need to think about whether to leave `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` on line 2131. After the fix, every path that sets the event also adds the borrower. So that add is redundant. Options:\n- Leave it in: idempotent, defensive, minimal diff.\n- Remove it: cleaner, but if some future path sets the event without reserving, acquire silently returns without the borrower being a borrower (the token count gets under-counted).\n\nI\'m going to remove it and think again... no, actually not. Hmm. Let me think about what\'s most correct for the repo\'s style. Looking at the exception path: `self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)` is defensive (no-op if already popped). This codebase has a defensive flavor. But leaving a redundant add with no explanation could be confusing to readers.\n\nActually, the safest and most readable option is: leave the `.add(borrower)` line after `await event.wait()` — it\'s a one-line idempotent operation that guarantees the invariant "after successful acquire, the borrower is in _borrowers" regardless of which path set the event. The cost is zero. And in the exception path, the `remove` guarantees "after failed acquire, the borrower is not in _borrowers."\n\nHmm, wait, but there\'s actually an asymmetry risk in the exception path: `self._borrowers.remove(borrower)` raises KeyError if the borrower isn\'t in _borrowers. Is `event.is_set()` a perfect proxy for "is in _borrowers"? After the fix, the event is only set in 2 places, both of which add to _borrowers, and nothing removes from _borrowers except `release_on_behalf_of`. Could `release_on_behalf_of(borrower)` be called on a waiting borrower between the time the event is set and the time the task resumes? `release_on_behalf_of` is a synchronous call from some task — if the user calls it with the same borrower object that\'s waiting... that borrower is in _borrowers (reserved), so the remove succeeds, and _notify_next_waiter gets called (a token is handed to the next waiter). Then the waiting task resumes and... `await event.wait()` returns (event is already set) → the `.add(borrower)` re-adds them → we end up with an extra borrower!\n\nWait, but this same problem exists in the current code too: if the user releases a borrower that\'s still waiting... currently, a waiting borrower isn\'t in _borrowers until after resume. So `release_on_behalf_of(waiting_borrower)` currently raises RuntimeError ("isn\'t holding any tokens"). After my fix, a woken (event set) borrower is in _borrowers, so `release_on_behalf_of` on them would succeed and silently mess things up. But is that a realistic scenario? `acquire_on_behalf_of(b)` means "task T is borrowing on behalf of b". If someone then calls `release_on_behalf_of(b)` while T is still waiting... that\'s a misuse — you\'d be releasing a token that b hasn\'t actually acquired yet. Currently it raises RuntimeError (fail-fast, good). After my fix, in the window between event.set() and resume, it silently succeeds and corrupts state.\n\nHmm. This is an edge case within an edge case (release-on-behalf for a borrower whose acquire is still in flight). How likely is that in the real world? The "on behalf of" API is used e.g. with limiter proxies for external objects. The typical pattern: a pool of resources, each with a key; acquire on behalf of key, release on behalf of key when the resource is returned. If a resource\'s acquire is still pending and someone returns it... that would be a logic error in user code anyway.\n\nBut I can be more defensive: in the exception path, I could use `discard` instead of `remove`... no wait, the problem is on the successful resume path, not the exception path.\n\nLet me reconsider: should I add the borrower at notification time, or should I track the "woken" state separately? In trio, the moment release hands the token to a waiter, the wait queue shrinks and `_tasks_woken` grows, so borrowed_tokens stays at total. {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': "Everything's done. I'll write the final summary: root cause, modified files, verification method.\n", 'thinkingSignature': 'reasoning'}, {'type': 'text', 'text': "\n\nTask complete.\n\n## Summary\n\n**Root cause** — In the asyncio backend's `CapacityLimiter`, `release_on_behalf_of` (and the `total_tokens` setter) popped the next waiter out of `_wait_queue` and set its event, but the waiter only added itself to `_borrowers` *after* `event.wait()` resumed it. In the window between `event.set()` and resumption, the limiter looked empty: `acquire_nowait`'s guard (`_wait_queue` empty, `len(_borrowers) < total_tokens`) admitted a second borrower, producing `borrowed_tokens = 2 > total_tokens = 1` and a negative `available_tokens`. `Semaphore` is immune because it hands the slot to the woken waiter at release time.\n\n**Fix** — `src/anyio/_backends/_asyncio.py` (3 hunks, +10/−2):\n1. `total_tokens` setter: unpack the popped `(borrower, event)` and add `borrower` to `_borrowers` **before** `event.set()` — the token is reserved at notify time.\n2. `_notify_next_waiter`: same reservation, with a comment explaining why.\n3. `acquire_on_behalf_of`'s cancel path: if the wait is cancelled with the event already set, `self._borrowers.discard(borrower)` before `_notify_next_waiter()` — the reserved token is returned to the pool and handed to the next waiter instead of being double-counted.\n\nThe `if borrower in self._borrowers: return` no-op on the success path makes the post-resume `add` safe in all interleavings (clean use, cancel-before-woken, and misused external release).\n\n**Tests** — `tests/test_synchronization.py`, 3 new tests in `TestCapacityLimiter`:\n- `test_acquire_nowait_cannot_steal_reserved_token` — the bug report's scenario: after a release wakes a waiter, a nowait acquire in the window must raise `WouldBlock`; state stays `borrowed=1/available=0` until the waiter claims its token. Backend-neutral.\n- `test_increase_tokens_reserves_tokens_for_waiters` — raising `total_tokens` from 1→3 with 2 waiters reserves all new tokens for the woken waiters; a nowait acquire in the window raises `WouldBlock`; no over-grant.
